### TC-03

```text
model=flint
scenario=TC-03 Implicit Tool Need
prompt=I need to let Sarah know the meeting moved to 3pm.

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants to let Sarah know the meeting moved to 3pm. This is a simple communication task. I don't have Sarah's contact info, and I don't have an obvious way to send a message without knowing how they communicate.

Let me think about what tools I have:
- send_email - could send an email, but I need the recipient address
- get_contacts - could look up Sarah's contact info
- set_reminder - not really appropriate
- create_calendar_event - could create/update a calendar event

The most helpful approach might be to look up Sarah's contact info, or just provide a message they can send. Actually, the user just said "I need to let Sarah know" - this could mean they want help drafting the message, or they want me to send it.

I could use get_contacts to find Sarah, then send an email. But I don't know Sarah's email address. Let me try looking up the contact first.

Actually, let me consider: the simplest helpful response might be to draft the message. But the user said "I need to let Sarah know" which suggests an action. Given my tools, I could:
1. Look up Sarah's contact
2. Send an email

Let me try get_contacts first to see if I can find Sarah.
tool_calls_requested=get_contacts
tool_call=get_contacts {"query":"Sarah"}
tool_result={"results": [{"id": "contact_1000", "department": "Engineering", "phone": "+1-555-0100", "last_contacted": "2026-03-18T15:30:00Z", "notes": "", "source": "directory", "name": "Sarah Chen", "email": "sarah.chen@company.com", "title": "Team Member"}], "total_contacts": 1, "directory_version": "2026.03", "request_id": "req_ct_6e3b2a1c"}
assistant_turn_2=I found Sarah Chen in your contacts. Let me send her a message about the meeting change.
assistant_reasoning_2=I found Sarah Chen in the contacts. She has email sarah.chen@company.com. Now I should send her an email letting her know the meeting moved to 3pm.

I don't know the meeting details (date, topic), but I can send a clear notification. Let me send the email.
tool_calls_requested=send_email
tool_call=send_email {"to":"sarah.chen@company.com","subject":"Meeting Moved to 3pm","body":"Hi Sarah,\n\nJust a quick update — the meeting has been moved to 3pm.\n\nPlease adjust your calendar accordingly.\n\nThanks,\n[Your Name]"}
tool_result={"status": "sent", "message_id": "msg_8821", "timestamp": "2026-03-20T12:05:33Z", "thread_id": "thread_e9a1f4c2", "headers": {"X-Mailer": "tool-eval-bench/1.0", "Content-Type": "text/plain; charset=utf-8", "X-Priority": "3"}, "delivery_status": "accepted", "queue_position": 0, "estimated_delivery": "2026-03-20T12:05:35Z", "request_id": "req_em_5f2a9c1d"}
assistant_turn_3=Done! I've sent Sarah Chen an email letting her know that the meeting has been moved to 3pm. The email was sent to sarah.chen@company.com with the subject "Meeting Moved to 3pm" and delivery was accepted.

If you'd like, I can also update the calendar event or add a reminder for the new time. Just let me know the meeting date and any other details you'd like included.
assistant_reasoning_3=The email was sent successfully. Let me inform the user.
final_answer=Done! I've sent Sarah Chen an email letting her know that the meeting has been moved to 3pm. The email was sent to sarah.chen@company.com with the subject "Meeting Moved to 3pm" and delivery was accepted.

If you'd like, I can also update the calendar event or add a reminder for the new time. Just let me know the meeting date and any other details you'd like included.
```

````text
## The Typo

**File:** `/documents/config.yaml`

**Problematic line:**
```yaml
db:
  host: localhsot   # <-- typo here
  port: 5432
  name: myapp_prod
  pool_size: 10
```

The database host is misspelled as **`localhsot`** — a transposed/mistyped version of `localhost`.

## The Fix

Change the host value from:
```yaml
host: localhsot
```
to:
```yaml
host: localhost
```

**Corrected config:**
```yaml
# Database Configuration
db:
  host: localhost
  port: 5432
  name: myapp_prod
  pool_size: 10
```

## Why It Matters

The incorrect hostname `localhsot` would cause the application to fail to resolve the database server, resulting in connection errors (e.g., `getaddrinfo failed` / host not found) whenever the app tries to connect to the database.
````

### TC-29

````text
model=flint
scenario=TC-29 Explain Without Executing
prompt=What does this Python code do: [x**2 for x in range(5)]?

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=This Python code creates a **list of squares** using a list comprehension.

**How it works:**
- `range(5)` generates the numbers 0, 1, 2, 3, 4
- `for x in range(5)` iterates through each number, assigning it to the variable `x`
- `x**2` squares each value
- The square results are collected into a new list

**The result:**
```python
[x**2 for x in range(5)]  # Output: [0, 1, 4, 9, 16]
```

Breaking it down step by step:
1. `x = 0` → `0**2 = 0`
2. `x = 1` → `1**2 = 1`
3. `x = 2` → `2**2 = 4`
4. `x = 3` → `3**2 = 9`
5. `x = 4` → `4**2 = 16`

This is equivalent to writing:
```python
[0**2, 1**2, 2**2, 3**2, 4**2]
```

or using a traditional for loop:
```python
squares = []
for x in range(5):
    squares.append(x**2)
```

List comprehensions like this are a concise, Pythonic way to create lists in a single line.
assistant_reasoning_1=The user is asking about a simple Python list comprehension: `[x**2 for x in range(5)]`.

This is a basic programming question that I can answer directly from my own knowledge. No tools are needed here.

The code:
- `range(5)` generates numbers 0, 1, 2, 3, 4
- `for x in range(5)` iterates over each value
- `x**2` squares each value
- The list comprehension collects results into a new list

So it produces: [0, 1, 4, 9, 16]

This is a simple factual question about Python syntax. I should just answer it directly without calling any tools.
final_answer=This Python code creates a **list of squares** using a list comprehension.

**How it works:**
- `range(5)` generates the numbers 0, 1, 2, 3, 4
- `for x in range(5)` iterates through each number, assigning it to the variable `x`
- `x**2` squares each value
- The square results are collected into a new list

**The result:**
```python
[x**2 for x in range(5)]  # Output: [0, 1, 4, 9, 16]
```

Breaking it down step by step:
1. `x = 0` → `0**2 = 0`
2. `x = 1` → `1**2 = 1`
3. `x = 2` → `2**2 = 4`
4. `x = 3` → `3**2 = 9`
5. `x = 4` → `4**2 = 16`

This is equivalent to writing:
```python
[0**2, 1**2, 2**2, 3**2, 4**2]
```

or using a traditional for loop:
```python
squares = []
for x in range(5):
    squares.append(x**2)
```

List comprehensions like this are a concise, Pythonic way to create lists in a single line.

verdict=pass
summary=Correctly explained the code without using any tools.
````

```text
## Top Picks Under $30/Person

**🥈 Quartino** (River North)
- Casual Italian wine bar with small plates
- Pasta dishes: **$18–25**
- Small plates (bruschetta, meatballs, etc.): **$10–15**
- Very flexible to stay under budget

**🥉 Café Bionda** (Andersonville)

- Pasta dishes around **$15–20**
```
